<a id="item-146"></a>
## [Context-Ready Transformer: RNN-Like Efficiency with Parallel Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27538) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The Context-Ready Transformer introduces a correction network that pre-contextualizes tokens before they enter a transformer block, enabling recurrent generation while maintaining parallel training. A 5-layer model outperforms a 12-layer standard transformer with 1.7x faster inference on an A100 GPU. This architecture bridges the gap between transformers and RNNs, offering faster inference without sacrificing quality, which is critical for deploying large language models in latency-sensitive applications. It also allows converting pretrained transformers with minimal fine-tuning, making it practical for existing models. The correction network is a zero-initialized feedforward network that combines the previous block output with the current token embedding. During training, the correction process is unrolled K times over the full sequence, enabling parallel processing at each step. A single-layer model with K=10 beats a 6-layer transformer with 2.6x speedup.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Standard transformers process all tokens in parallel during training but suffer from quadratic attention cost and sequential generation. Recurrent neural networks (RNNs) have linear inference cost but cannot be trained in parallel. The Context-Ready Transformer combines the best of both: parallel training via unrolling and efficient recurrent inference via a correction network.

This work advances real-time freeway safety monitoring by providing interpretable per-vehicle conflict type attribution, which is critical for autonomous driving and traffic management systems. HIA-GAT processes longitudinal and lateral interactions through separate attention pathways and fuses them via a conflict-type-aware gating mechanism with event-level supervision from SSM conflict attribution.

## [KARMA: Global XAI for Time Series via Markov Approximations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27599) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced KARMA, a method that constructs a K-order Markov surrogate model to provide global explanations for multivariate time series forecasting models, addressing the temporal dependency issue ignored by existing XAI methods. This work fills a critical gap in explainable AI for time series forecasting, where temporal dependencies are fundamental. KARMA enables users to understand and trust predictions in domains like weather, finance, and healthcare. KARMA identifies the minimal history length K that is predictively sufficient, estimates the best-fitting K-order Markov transition kernel from discretized history, and derives a five-level global explanation hierarchy. It outperforms TimeSHAP in capturing temporal dependencies on synthetic data.

**Background**: Explainable AI (XAI) methods aim to make black-box models interpretable. However, most XAI techniques assume independent data points, which is invalid for time series due to temporal dependencies. K-order Markov models approximate a process where the next state depends only on the previous K states, making them suitable for capturing such dependencies.

## [Probe-Based Uncertainty Estimation in LLMs: A Factorised Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27679) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a factorised study of probe-based uncertainty estimation for LLMs, revealing that raw hidden states and attention features perform well in-domain but structured/compressed features are more robust under distribution shift. It also shows that prompting and label construction significantly affect probe behavior. These findings provide crucial guidance for deploying probe-based uncertainty estimators in real-world LLM applications, where distribution shifts are common. The work encourages more deployment-oriented evaluation and offers a stable off-the-shelf baseline for hallucination detection. The study systematically compares raw hidden states, attention features, structured features, and compressed features under matched conditions. They also train benchmark-based pretrained probes that transfer reasonably well to open-ended factual generation.

SMMD adapts MMD to handle numeric tokens by incorporating value-distance kernels and graph smoothness to encourage local consistency in predictions.

RS-Diffuser introduces a risk-sensitive offline diffusion planning framework that combines trajectory generation with distributional value critics, enabling flexible risk-averse, risk-neutral, or risk-seeking behaviors by adjusting a single inference-time parameter. Researchers propose Activation Amplification and Attenuation (A3), a lightweight plug-in module that dynamically rescales activations using a learnable mask and scaling factor to enhance adversarial robustness. A3 introduces contrastive and ranking losses by amplifying adversarial signals as negative references, and achieves robustness by simply flipping the sign of the scaling operation. A3 offers a simple yet effective approach to adversarial defense with minimal computational overhead, making it practical for real-world deployment. Its plug-and-play nature and consistent improvements across backbones and datasets could strengthen security in deep learning systems. A3 uses only a small number of learnable parameters, with most behavior determined by the scaling mechanism rather than additional network capacity. Experiments show that integrating A3 into different backbones, datasets, and training methods consistently improves adversarial robustness while introducing negligible computational and memory overhead.

**Background**: Adversarial attacks exploit non-robust features in neural networks by adding small perturbations to inputs, causing misclassifications. Prior defenses often rely on pruning, masking, or feature recalibration, but many incur significant computational cost or degrade clean accuracy. A3 addresses this by learning to amplify and attenuate activation signals through a simple scaling mechanism, leveraging contrastive and ranking losses to improve robustness without heavy modifications.

## [ROCKET-ActCost: Aligning Allocation Cost with Output Objective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27785) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study proposes ROCKET-ActCost, which aligns the allocation cost in ROCKET-based LLM compression with the output-space objective instead of weight-space Frobenius error. On Qwen3-8B at 50% compression, it achieves 53.1% average accuracy (vs 52.3%) but increases WikiText perplexity by 16%. This reveals a tradeoff between accuracy and perplexity in calibration-guided LLM compression, showing that different allocation objectives favor different downstream metrics. It provides valuable insights for practitioners choosing compression strategies. The high correlation (>0.99) between weight-space and output-space errors limits allocation divergence, explaining the modest effect size. On Llama-3.2-1B at 20% compression, the two methods produce near-identical results, suggesting the cost function effect is minor at lower compression ratios.

**Background**: ROCKET is a training-free compression method that combines sparse-dictionary factorization with multi-choice knapsack problem (MCKP) allocation. It uses calibration data to guide compression decisions, deriving per-layer factorization from an output reconstruction objective but originally using weight-space Frobenius error as the MCKP allocation cost.

## [NLL-Guided Layer Selection Boosts Hybrid Attention Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27791) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a training-free method that selects which layers in a hybrid attention model should use full attention by measuring negative log-likelihood (NLL) degradation, achieving 64.6% accuracy on LongMemEval with only 1/4 full-attention layers, matching the 1/2 periodic baseline while halving compute. This method advances the efficiency-accuracy Pareto frontier for long-context LLM deployment, enabling significant computational savings without sacrificing accuracy. It addresses a practical bottleneck in deploying large language models for tasks requiring long context windows. The method requires only about 15 minutes of one-time calibration on a single GPU. It outperforms the SWAA-reported periodic 1/4-FA baseline by 10.4 percentage points and a LightTransfer-style baseline by 26.4 percentage points on LongMemEval with Qwen3-4B.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Hybrid attention models combine full attention and sliding-window attention across layers to reduce computational cost in long-context inference. However, deciding which layers should retain full attention is non-trivial; existing heuristics like fixed periodic patterns may not capture layer-specific importance. NLL degradation measures the increase in negative log-likelihood when a layer's full attention is replaced with sliding-window attention, directly indicating its importance for downstream accuracy.

## [Position Bias Correction Alone Fails to Match Iterative Attention Sorting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27793) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proposes Debiased One-Pass Attention Sorting to correct position bias in long-context LLMs, but experiments show it closes only 37% of the performance gap compared to iterative attention sorting. This finding challenges the assumption that position bias is the primary bottleneck in attention sorting, suggesting that iterative reordering provides additional benefits beyond bias correction, which has implications for efficient long-context processing. On LLaMA-2-7B-32K-Instruct, debiasing produced identical results to uncalibrated single-pass sorting (94.83% containment accuracy), while on YaRN-Llama-2-7b-64k, it improved accuracy by 8.67 percentage points but remained 14.84 points behind iterative sorting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context language models often exhibit position bias, where information in the middle of the context is underutilized. Attention Sorting is a technique that iteratively reorders documents based on attention patterns to improve performance, but it requires multiple sort-and-generate cycles, increasing cost. The paper hypothesizes that position bias is the main bottleneck and proposes a single-pass correction method, but finds it insufficient.

Pepti-drift is a novel latent refinement framework that generates antigen-specific binding peptides while repelling toxicity-associated regions in the embedding space, using a warm-up strategy to balance binding attraction and toxicity repulsion. This work addresses a critical challenge in therapeutic peptide discovery by explicitly avoiding toxicity during generation, potentially accelerating the development of safer peptide-based drugs. The framework operates in a learned peptide embedding space, where it attracts generated latents toward antigen-matched binding peptides and repels them from toxicity-associated regions, with a warm-up phase that first focuses on binding attraction before increasing toxicity repulsion.

**Background**: Peptides are promising therapeutics combining small-molecule tunability with macromolecular specificity, but designing antigen-specific binding peptides without toxicity is challenging because binding and toxicity features often overlap in representation space. ## [Parallel Rollout Approximation Boosts Pixel-Space AR Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27978) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Parallel Rollout Approximation (PRA), a scalable framework that generates low-dimensional intermediate states to reduce error accumulation and train-inference gap in pixel-space autoregressive image generation. PRA-S with 135M parameters achieves FID 2.58 on ImageNet-1K 256x256, surpassing previous billion-scale pixel-space AR models, and PRA-L with 511M parameters sets a new state-of-the-art FID of 1.94. PRA addresses two fundamental challenges in pixel-space autoregressive generation—high-dimensional patch error and train-inference mismatch—without requiring discrete tokenizers or separate pretrained components. This could enable more unified and scalable generative models for pixel-space image understanding and generation, potentially impacting fields like computer vision and generative AI. PRA generates low-dimensional intermediate states and maps them back to pixel-space tokens via a pixel decoder, preserving a pixel-in, pixel-out interface. It constructs inference-like pixel inputs through the same intermediate-state-to-pixel path used at inference, enabling parallel teacher-forced training while approximating rollout conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Pixel-space autoregressive (AR) generation models images as sequences of raw pixel patches, avoiding discrete tokenization. However, it suffers from large single-step errors due to high-dimensional patch generation and a train-inference gap caused by teacher forcing, where training uses ground-truth inputs but inference uses predicted ones. Existing fixes like x-prediction and input noise injection only partially mitigate these issues, while exact rollout training is too slow.

## [OSOR: One-Step Diffusion Inpainting for Object Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28094) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OSOR introduces a one-step diffusion inpainting method that efficiently removes objects while handling shadows, reflections, and imperfect masks, achieving 4× to 30× faster inference than multi-step diffusion baselines. This work enables real-time interactive object removal on edge devices, overcoming the high computational cost of traditional diffusion models. It also addresses practical challenges like non-local effects and mask inaccuracies, making it suitable for real-world applications. OSOR introduces three key components: an occupancy-guided discriminator for stable single-step training, an alpha head to handle imperfect masks, and a semantic-anchored verification pipeline (SAVP) to curate the CORNE dataset with 280K verified removal pairs. The method also includes AnimeEraseBench and TextEraseBench for evaluation on complex tasks.

## [BiDeMem: Bidirectional Memory for Explainable Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BiDeMem proposes a bidirectional degradation memory that retrieves compact top-k memory slots from restoration features and input statistics, using the same slot identity for both restoration and a training-only forward-degradation explanation path. This work addresses the lack of interpretability in degradation-aware image restoration methods by providing a falsifiable explanation mechanism, enabling researchers to verify whether the model truly understands degradation rather than exploiting shortcuts. In controlled experiments on NAFNet, variants without the full bidirectional memory (correction head only, dense query prior, static global prior) lag behind BiDeMem by 0.2588, 0.2586, and 0.2839 dB respectively, and intervention probes show BiDeMem increases sensitivity to wrong and native priors while preserving restoration quality.

BiDeMem introduces a memory mechanism that retrieves explicit degradation slots, making the reasoning process auditable.

Researchers propose ADC-GNN, an attention-guided diffusion-contrastive graph neural network that combines feature-space diffusion augmentation, contrastive learning, and multi-hop spectral attention for few-shot graph fraud detection under sparse labels and camouflaged anomalies. Researchers propose Concept-Guided In-Context Segmentation (CG-ICS), a new paradigm that uses a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to extract high-level semantic concepts from reference images, combined with a SAM3-driven scoring function and tree-search refinement, to improve robustness and accuracy in in-context segmentation. This work addresses the overlooked issue of robustness in in-context segmentation, where prior methods produce inconsistent results under different reference choices. By introducing concept guidance, CG-ICS achieves state-of-the-art accuracy with significantly reduced variance, making ICS systems more reliable for real-world applications. CG-ICS uses a concept reasoning module with an MLLM to propose candidate concepts and a SAM3-driven scoring function with tree-search refinement to select reliable textual concepts, alongside a parallel visual exemplar route for query-side spatial grounding. The method operates on a frozen SAM3 backbone and achieves state-of-the-art results on standard ICS benchmarks.

Researchers propose DEFAR (DirEctional-Frequency Adaptive Rectification), a framework that uses directional and frequency feedback from exposure bias itself to improve flow matching models. It introduces Anti-Drift Rectification (ADR) and Frequency Compensation (FC) to enhance bias tolerance. This work offers a novel self-rectification approach to exposure bias, a key challenge in flow matching generative models. It could lead to more robust and higher-quality generation across image, video, and other domains. DEFAR simulates single-step inference during training to identify exposure bias, then uses the bias itself as feedback. Experiments on CIFAR-10, CelebA-64, and ImageNet-256/512 show DEFAR outperforms prior baselines and demonstrates scalability and inference robustness.

Existing methods use static constraints or external heuristics, but DEFAR leverages the bias itself for dynamic rectification.

## [SIGA: Lightweight Adapter Auto-Configures Scientific Simulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09774) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SIGA, a lightweight adapter that enables off-the-shelf coding agents to auto-configure scientific simulators by supplying the simulator's executable contract through retrieval, procedural memory, validation, and validation-gated termination. This approach reduces a persistent bottleneck in scientific computing—configuring simulators—from hours to minutes, and improves reliability by preventing empty or invalid input decks, potentially accelerating simulation-driven research across domains. On the GEOS multiphysics simulator, SIGA improved TreeSim's accuracy from 0.720 to 0.789 on hard tasks and reduced across-run standard deviation by about 16x. In a human calibration, SIGA achieved in about five minutes the deck quality a domain expert reached in about three hours.

This process is a persistent bottleneck that can cost domain scientists hours to days. Coding agents already excel at file navigation, code editing, and command execution, but lack the simulator's executable contract. SIGA provides that contract externally, allowing the agent to self-evolve by rewriting adapter contents from prior trajectories.

This paper characterizes the minimal set of nodes guaranteed to contain the optimal conditional intervention in causal bandits and proposes an O(|V|+|E|) algorithm to identify it. This work significantly reduces the search space for optimal interventions, accelerating convergence in bandit algorithms and making conditional interventions more practical for real-world decision-making. The algorithm runs in linear time relative to the graph size and is proven correct. Empirical results show it prunes the search space and improves convergence rates when integrated with standard multi-armed bandit algorithms.

Prior work focused on hard interventions, which fix a variable to a constant value regardless of context.

ReFreeKV proposes a threshold-free KV cache compression method that adaptively allocates memory budget without requiring input-specific thresholds, achieving lossless compression across diverse datasets. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of existing KV cache pruning methods—their dependence on input-specific thresholds—which hinders deployment in real-world open-domain scenarios. By eliminating threshold tuning, ReFreeKV enables more robust and practical memory reduction for LLM inference. ReFreeKV is evaluated on 13 datasets with varying context lengths, task types, and model sizes, demonstrating efficacy and efficiency. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: During autoregressive inference, large language models (LLMs) cache key and value tensors (KV cache) to avoid recomputation, but this cache grows linearly with sequence length, causing memory bottlenecks. Existing KV cache pruning methods reduce memory by evicting less important tokens, but they often require manually setting a threshold per input or domain to decide how many tokens to keep, which is impractical for diverse real-world inputs.

## [Unbiased Binning for Fair Data Discretization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21785) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced the unbiased binning problem to minimize bias in discretized features while maintaining group parity, and developed a dynamic programming algorithm to solve it. They also proposed epsilon-biased binning with a scalable local search algorithm for practical use. This work addresses a critical but overlooked source of bias in data preprocessing, which can amplify unfairness in downstream machine learning models. It provides theoretical guarantees and efficient algorithms, making fairness-aware discretization practical for real-world datasets. The unbiased binning problem finds the closest discretization to equal-size binning that satisfies group parity across buckets. The dynamic programming algorithm runs in quadratic time, while the local search algorithm uses a divide-and-conquer approach for near-linear time performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Discretization (binning) is a common data preprocessing step that converts continuous features into buckets, but it can introduce bias if different groups are distributed unevenly across buckets. Group parity requires that the proportion of each group in every bucket matches the overall population proportion. The price of fairness refers to the loss in utility (e.g., deviation from equal-size binning) incurred to achieve fairness.

## [Constraint Automata Achieve Optimal EXPTIME for DLs with Concrete Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19644) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces constraint automata to solve the consistency problem for description logics with concrete domains, achieving an optimal EXPTIME upper bound. The approach is shown to be robust across extensions including inverse roles, functional roles, and constraint assertions. This result provides a theoretically optimal complexity bound for a fundamental reasoning problem in description logics with concrete domains, which are widely used in ontology languages like OWL. The automata-based approach offers a uniform framework that can simplify future complexity analyses and guide practical algorithm design. The constraint automata enrich transitions with symbolic constraints, and their nonemptiness problem is shown to be in EXPTIME when concrete domains satisfy a few simple properties. The reduction from ontology consistency to automata nonemptiness yields EXPTIME-membership, matching the known lower bound.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Description logics (DLs) are a family of knowledge representation languages used to model ontologies. Concrete domains allow DLs to refer to concrete objects like numbers and strings, enabling reasoning about quantitative constraints. The consistency problem asks whether an ontology has a model, and its complexity is a key theoretical question. Prior approaches used tableaux or type elimination, but automata-based methods offer a more modular and extensible framework.

## [GRACE: Efficient INT4 VLMs via Distillation and QAT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22709) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose GRACE, a framework that unifies knowledge distillation and quantization-aware training under the Information Bottleneck principle, producing INT4 vision-language models that outperform FP16 baselines on benchmarks like SQA and MMBench. GRACE enables deployment of high-performance VLMs on resource-constrained devices by achieving 3x throughput and 54% memory reduction with minimal accuracy loss, addressing a key bottleneck in VLM adoption. The framework introduces confidence-gated decoupled distillation to filter unreliable supervision, relational centered kernel alignment to transfer visual token structures, and an adaptive controller via Lagrangian relaxation to balance fidelity and capacity constraints.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding but are computationally expensive. Post-training quantization often degrades accuracy, while quantization-aware training (QAT) simulates low-precision effects during training to maintain performance. The Information Bottleneck principle provides a theoretical framework for compressing input data while preserving task-relevant information.

## [SpecTF: Frequency-Aware Multimodal Time-Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01588) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose SpecTF, a framework that integrates textual data into time-series forecasting by decomposing time series into frequency components and fusing text embeddings via cross-attention in the spectral domain. This approach addresses the limitation of existing methods that neglect multiscale temporal influences of text, achieving state-of-the-art performance with fewer parameters, which is crucial for real-world applications like finance and weather forecasting. SpecTF uses spectral decomposition to separate time series into short-term and long-term frequency components, then applies a lightweight cross-attention mechanism to reweight these components based on textual relevance. Experiments show significant improvements over state-of-the-art models on diverse multimodal datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal time-series forecasting combines numerical data with contextual signals like text. Traditional methods align text and time series at each time step, missing global patterns such as cycles and trends. Spectral decomposition breaks a time series into frequency bands, capturing both fast and slow variations, which helps integrate textual context more effectively.

HiMu is a training-free framework that decomposes compositional queries into a hierarchical logic tree for efficient multimodal frame selection in long video question answering, achieving state-of-the-art accuracy under a 16-frame budget. This addresses a key bottleneck in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for long video understanding, enabling more accurate and efficient frame selection without retraining, which can improve video QA systems and reduce computational costs. HiMu uses a single text-only LLM call to decompose queries into a logic tree with atomic predicates, which are evaluated by lightweight experts for vision (CLIP, detection, OCR) and audio (speech recognition, sound matching), then combined via fuzzy-logic operators to produce a per-frame satisfaction curve.

## [FormalASR: Direct Spoken Chinese to Formal Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19266) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FormalASR introduces two compact end-to-end models (0.6B and 1.7B parameters) that directly transcribe spoken Chinese into formal written text without requiring a separate LLM for post-editing, achieving up to 37.4% relative CER reduction over verbatim baselines. This work addresses the gap between verbatim ASR and downstream writing applications by providing a lightweight, on-device solution that reduces latency and memory cost. It enables practical deployment of spoken-to-formal transcription in resource-constrained environments. The models are fine-tuned from Qwen3-ASR using two newly constructed datasets, WenetSpeech-Formal and Speechio-Formal, which are created via LLM-based rewriting and quality filtering. FormalASR also improves ROUGE-L and BERTScore compared to verbatim baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional ASR systems output verbatim transcriptions that include disfluencies and filler words, which are often unsuitable for writing. A common solution is a two-stage pipeline using an LLM for post-editing, but this increases latency and memory, making on-device deployment difficult. FormalASR combines ASR and formalization into a single model, eliminating the need for an external LLM at inference time.

## [LLM Warm-Start for HPO Is Just a Default Config](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21641) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A budget-matched study on eight PMLB tabular benchmarks shows that an LLM advisor for hyperparameter optimization provides no significant advantage over classical baselines, as the warm-start benefit comes from a fixed default configuration, not the LLM itself. This finding challenges the claimed benefits of LLMs for hyperparameter optimization, with implications for the AutoML community: practitioners should seed classical search with a sensible default rather than rely on an LLM advisor. The LLM's own proposals added only +0.40 pp of cross-validation accuracy over the seed and nothing on held-out test; classical search without the seed ties the advisor by 12 evaluations and beats it by 40 evaluations (+0.6 to +0.8 pp, p ≤ 1e-4).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Hyperparameter optimization (HPO) is the process of tuning machine learning model hyperparameters to maximize performance. Large language models (LLMs) have been proposed as advisors that can warm-start HPO by suggesting good configurations from prior knowledge. This study uses a budget-matched protocol to fairly compare an LLM advisor against classical methods like random search, Optuna-TPE, Gaussian-process Bayesian optimization, and successive halving on tabular benchmarks from the PMLB suite.

Yuvion VL is a family of multimodal large language models purpose-built for content and AI safety, using adversarial-aware data synthesis and a three-stage training pipeline. This work directly addresses the critical gap where general-purpose models fail to reliably identify real-world multimodal risks. By treating safety as an adversarial problem, Yuvion VL sets a new standard for robust AI safety evaluation and could influence future safety regulations and model deployment practices. The model introduces Confuse-then-Contrast Fine-Tuning, a contrastive framework that mines model-specific confusions to enforce discrimination of fine-grained visual-semantic elements. Researchers propose DiARC, a method that uses preference alignment with negative samples to improve large language model reasoning on Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus (ARC) tasks, outperforming standard supervised fine-tuning. This work addresses a key limitation in LLM-based reasoning on ARC tasks by incorporating negative sample discrimination, which is crucial for neuro-symbolic reasoning and could lead to more robust AI systems. Experiments across multiple ARC-like benchmarks show consistent improvements.

## [RelBall: Quaternion Rotation for Knowledge Graph Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27967) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RelBall extends Rotate3D by introducing modulus transformation to model semantic hierarchies and a tail-centric relation ball to handle one-to-many, many-to-one, and many-to-many relations, achieving coverage of all relational patterns including non-commutative composition. This work addresses key limitations of existing knowledge graph embedding models, enabling more accurate link prediction for complex relational patterns, which is crucial for applications like question answering and recommendation systems. RelBall uses quaternion rotations for non-commutativity and adjusts entity moduli to reflect semantic levels: abstract concepts have smaller moduli, concrete instances larger. It also introduces a relation ball centered at the tail entity to model cardinality constraints.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graph completion (KGC) aims to predict missing links in knowledge graphs using known triples (head, relation, tail). Existing models like RotatE use complex number rotations for relational patterns but fail at non-commutative composition and hierarchy. Rotate3D extended rotations to 3D but still cannot model hierarchies or one-to-many relations. RelBall builds on these by combining modulus transformation with quaternion rotation.

A new distribution-based multiple instance learning framework using zero-inflated beta parameters is proposed to predict tumor proportion score (TPS) in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) from slide-level labels only. Accurate TPS assessment is critical for NSCLC treatment planning, and this method reduces the need for tedious manual annotations while improving prediction accuracy and explainability. The framework consists of two models: an embedding-extraction and multiclass-classification network for patch features, and a MIL model that aggregates embeddings to predict zero-inflated beta distribution parameters for the slide-level TPS.

**Background**: Multiple instance learning (MIL) is a machine learning paradigm where models are trained on bags of instances (e.g., patches from whole slide images) with only bag-level labels. The zero-inflated beta distribution is a mixture distribution that models continuous values between 0 and 1 with an additional probability mass at zero, suitable for fractional data like TPS scores.

## [Quantum-Inspired Models for Efficient Traffic Matrix Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27821) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces compact quantum-inspired recurrent models, specifically gated quantum-inspired Kolmogorov-Arnold network fast-weight programmers (QKAN-FWPs), for traffic matrix forecasting under resource constraints. The best variant, G-QKANFWP, achieves lower RMSE than a larger LSTM while using only 22.4% of its parameters. This work demonstrates that quantum-inspired models can match or exceed classical recurrent networks in accuracy while being far more parameter-efficient, which is crucial for online network control with limited memory and compute. It opens a new direction for resource-conscious traffic engineering without relying on heavy graph or transformer modules. The models predict 20 future five-minute frames of a 144-channel origin-destination matrix from a two-hour history using the Abilene dataset. G-QKANFWP outperforms both a matched-size LSTM and a classical gated fast-weight programmer, showing the gain is not solely due to the fast-weight framework.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Traffic matrices capture network-wide origin-destination demand and are essential for traffic engineering, but forecasting them under online constraints is challenging. Quantum-inspired models use concepts from quantum computing (e.g., variational circuits) to achieve expressive power with fewer parameters. Fast weight programmers are meta-architectures where a slow controller dynamically programs fast weights for rapid adaptation.

Accurate network traffic prediction is crucial for efficient resource allocation in dynamic urban cellular networks, and PEHT demonstrates a parameter-efficient approach that outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, potentially enabling more scalable and cost-effective deployment. ## [iCost: Adaptive Instance-Complexity Penalties for Imbalanced Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13007) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose iCost, a cost-sensitive learning framework that assigns adaptive penalties to minority-class samples based on their estimated learning difficulty, using two complementary complexity estimation strategies: Neighbor-iCost and Gini-iCost. This work addresses a key limitation of conventional cost-sensitive learning—uniform penalization of all minority instances—which can introduce bias and degrade performance. By adapting penalties to instance complexity, iCost improves classification fairness and accuracy, offering a practical tool for imbalanced classification tasks. iCost is released as a scikit-learn-compatible Python package on PyPI. It was evaluated on 65 binary and 10 multiclass imbalanced datasets, outperforming conventional cost-sensitive learning and remaining competitive with resampling methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Class imbalance occurs when one class has significantly fewer samples than others, causing models to be biased toward the majority class. Cost-sensitive learning addresses this by assigning higher misclassification costs to minority-class errors, but typically uses a uniform cost for all minority instances, ignoring differences in sample difficulty. Instance-level cost-sensitive methods adapt costs per sample, but prior approaches often lacked a principled way to estimate instance complexity.

## [EAGT: 29 Augmentations Tested for Echo Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16427) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The study systematically evaluates 29 data augmentation techniques and their pairwise combinations for 2D left ventricular segmentation using a U-Net across three echocardiography datasets (Unity, CAMUS, EchoNet Dynamic). This provides empirical guidance for designing augmentation policies that improve cross-dataset generalizability in echocardiography segmentation, which is critical for clinical deployment of deep learning models. Geometry-based augmentations (affine, shift-scale-rotate, flip, perspective) yielded the largest and most consistent gains, while aggressive intensity- and artefact-based transforms often degraded transfer. Pairwise combinations outperformed individual augmentations mainly when complementary.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 29, 04:00

**Background**: Deep learning models for echocardiography segmentation often fail to generalize across institutions and scanners due to limited annotated data. Data augmentation is a low-cost technique to improve robustness, but its role in cross-dataset generalizability was not well understood. This study fills that gap by evaluating 29 augmentations on three public datasets.
